. As an example, when
V=(10,10,30,40,40)
,
R^{*}_{-}(2)=R^{*}_{+}(2)=2
— the index of
30
which is not at tie with any other numbers — whereas
R^{*}_{-}(0)=0<R^{*}_{+}(0)=1
— the starting and ending indices of
10
, as there are two numbers
10
at a tie. Also note the useful property that for
k,k^{\prime}\in[B]_{0}
,

\displaystyle R^{*}_{+}(k)\;<\;k^{\prime}\quad\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle V^{*}_{k}\;<\;V^{*}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\quad k\;<\;R^{*}_{-}(k^{\prime})\;.

(25)

The next result generalises
Theorem

2
to an arbitrary tie-breaking function
T
in (
).

Theorem 7
.

Let
f
be an
\mathcal{X}_{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
function for a generic measurable space
\mathcal{Y}
equipped with total order, and
T
be a random
\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}\cup\{0\}
tie-breaking function independent of all other variables such that
T(n)\in[0,n]
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
. For
r\in(0,1]
, additionally denote
B_{r}=\lceil r(B+1)\rceil-1
and

\displaystyle C_{T}(r)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R^*_-(B_r)}{B + 1}$}}+{\mathbb{P}}\Big(T(R^{*}_{+}(B_{r})-R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})+1)<r(B+1)-R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})\,\big|\,\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\,\times\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R^*_+(B_r) - R^*_-(B_r) + 1}{B + 1}$}}\;,

and use the convention that
B_{0}=C_{T}(0)=0
.
If
(f(\Psi_{0}(X)),\ldots,f(\Psi_{B}(X)))
is conditionally exchangeable given
{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}
, then for any
r\in[0,1]
, almost surely,

\displaystyle\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Phi}(X)}{B+1}$}}\,<\,r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,-\,\mathbb{E}[C_{T}(r)\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}]\,\Big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\Delta^{r}_{\rm Kol}({\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})\;.

C.2. Proof of
Theorem

2

We shall apply
Theorem

7
with
\mathcal{Y}=\mathbb{R}
and calculate
C_{T}
under the two choices of tie-breaking. Note that for either tie-breaking function, when
r=0
, we trivially have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[C_{T}(0)|{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}]\;=\;0\;=\;r\quad\text{ almost surely. }

For uniform tie-breaking,
T(n)\sim\text{Uniform}\{0,\ldots,n\}
. Then for
r\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle C_{T}(r)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R^*_-(B_r)}{B + 1}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lceil r(B+1) - R^*_-(B_r) \rceil}{R^*_+(B_r) - R^*_-(B_r) + 2}$}}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R^*_+(B_r) - R^*_-(B_r) + 1}{B + 1}$}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lceil r(B+1) \rceil}{B + 1}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B_r + 1 - R^*_-(B_r)}{R^*_+(B_r) - R^*_-(B_r) + 2}$}}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B + 1}$}}\qquad\text{ almost surely }\;.

In
(a)
above, we have noted that since
R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})
takes integer values,
R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})+\lceil r(B+1)-R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})\rceil=\lceil r(B+1)\rceil
, as well as the notation that
B_{r}=\lceil r(B+1)\rceil-1
. Now since, by construction,
R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})\leq B_{r}\leq R^{*}_{+}(B_{r})
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B_r + 1 - R^*_-(B_r)}{R^*_+(B_r) - R^*_-(B_r) + 2}$}}\;\in\;[0,1]\;,

and also note that
\lceil r(B+1)\rceil-r(B+1)\in[0,1]
. This implies that

\displaystyle\big|C_{T}(r)-r\big|\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B+1}$}}\qquad\text{ almost surely }\;,

and by
Theorem

7
, we obtain

\displaystyle\;\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Phi}(X)}{B+1}$}}\,<\,r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,-\,r\,\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Phi}(X)}{B+1}$}}\,<\,r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,-\,\mathbb{E}[C_{T}(r)\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}]\,\Big|+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B+1}$}}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\Delta^{r}_{\rm Kol}({\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B+1}$}}\;.

For smoothed uniform tie breaking,
T(n)\sim\text{Uniform}[0,n]
, so

\displaystyle C_{T}(r)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R^*_-(B_r)}{B + 1}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
r(B+1) - R^*_-(B_r)
}{
R^*_+(B_r) - R^*_-(B_r) + 1
}$}}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R^*_+(B_r) - R^*_-(B_r) + 1}{B + 1}$}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;r\quad\text{ almost surely.}

By
Theorem

7
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Phi}(X)}{B+1}$}}\,<\,r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,-\,r\,\Big|\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Phi}(X)}{B+1}$}}\,<\,r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,-\,\mathbb{E}[C_{T}(r)\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}]\,\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\Delta^{r}_{\rm Kol}({\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})\;.

This finishes the proof.

∎

C.3. Proof of
Theorem

7

Recall the shorthands that

\displaystyle R_{\Phi}(X)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;{\rm Rank}_{T}\big(f(X)\,;\,(f(\Phi_{b}(X)))_{b\in[B]}\big)\;,

\displaystyle R_{\Psi}(X)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;{\rm Rank}_{T}\big(f(\Psi_{0}(X))\,;\,(f(\Psi_{b}(X)))_{b\in[B]}\big)\;,

\displaystyle\Delta^{r}_{\rm Kol}({\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Phi}(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,-\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Psi}(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,\Big|\;.

Therefore by the triangle inequality, the quantity to control can be bounded as

\displaystyle\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Phi}(X)}{B+1}$}}\,<\,

\displaystyle r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,-\,\mathbb{E}\big[C_{T}(r)\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big]\,\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\;\Delta^{r}_{\rm Kol}({\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})\,+\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Psi}(X)}{B+1}$}}\,<\,r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,-\,\mathbb{E}\big[C_{T}(r)\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big]\,\Big|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Delta^{r}_{\rm Kol}({\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})\,+\,\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Psi}(X)}{B+1}$}}\,<\,r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\,,\,\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\Big)\,-\,C_{T}(r)\,\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]\,\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\Delta^{r}_{\rm Kol}({\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})\,+\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\;\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Psi}(X)}{B+1}$}}\,<\,r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\,,\,\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\Big)\,-\,C_{T}(r)\,\Big|\;\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]\;,

where we noted that
C_{T}(r)
is
\lbag V\rbag
-measurable. The main efforts of this proof focus on showing that, almost surely

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Psi}(X)}{B+1}$}}\,<\,r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\,,\,\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\Big)\;=\;C_{T}(r)\;.

(26)

The proof strategy adapts the idea of Lemma 8.7 of
86
and extends it from smoothed uniform tie-breaking to general tie-breaking. To this end, it is convenient to denote the ranks

\displaystyle R_{-}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{b\in[B]_{0}}\mathbb{I}_{\{V_{0}>V_{b}\}}\;=\;\msum_{b\in[B]}\mathbb{I}_{\{V_{0}>V_{b}\}}\;,

\displaystyle R_{+}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{b\in[B]_{0}}\mathbb{I}_{\{V_{0}\geq V_{b}\}}-1\;=\;\msum_{b\in[B]}\mathbb{I}_{\{V_{0}\geq V_{b}\}}\;,

where we have used
[B]_{0}=[B]\cup\{0\}
above. With this notation, we can express

\displaystyle R_{\Psi}(X)\;=\;{\rm Rank}_{T}\big(V_{0}\,;\,(V_{b})_{b\in[B]}\big)\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{b\in[B]}\mathbb{I}_{\{V_{0}>V_{b}\}}+T\Big(\msum_{b\in[B]}\mathbb{I}_{\{V_{0}=V_{b}\}}+1\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;R_{-}+T(R_{+}-R_{-}+1)\;.

Since
T(n)\leq n
by assumption,
R_{\Psi}(X)\in[R_{-},R_{+}+1]
. The goal is to compute, for
r\in[0,1]
, the quantity

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(R_{\Psi}(

\displaystyle X)<r(B+1)\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag})

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(\,R_{\Psi}(X)\leq R_{+}+1<r(B+1)\;\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\big)

\displaystyle\;+{\mathbb{P}}\big(\,R_{-}\leq R_{\Psi}(X)<r(B+1)\leq R_{+}+1\;\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\big)

\displaystyle\;\overset{(b)}{=}

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(\,R_{+}<r(B+1)-1\;\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\big)

\displaystyle\;+\mathbb{E}\Big[{\mathbb{P}}(R_{\Psi}(X)<r(B+1)\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag},R_{-},R_{+})\,\mathbb{I}_{\{R_{-}<r(B+1)\leq R_{+}+1\}}\;\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\Big]

\displaystyle\;\overset{(c)}{=}

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(\,R_{+}<r(B+1)-1\;\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\big)

\displaystyle\;\qquad+\mathbb{E}\Big[\;p_{T}\big(r(B+1)-R_{-}\,,\,R_{+}-R_{-}+1\big)\,\mathbb{I}_{\{R_{-}<r(B+1)\leq R_{+}+1\}}\;\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\Big]\;.

(27)

Note that we have used
R_{-}\leq R_{\Psi}(X)\leq R_{+}+1
almost surely in
(a)
and the tower rule in
(b)
; in
(c)
, we have taken an expectation over the randomness in
T
and written

\displaystyle p_{T}(x\,,\,n)\;\coloneqq\;{\mathbb{P}}(T(n)<x)\;.

Also note that in the case
r=0
, we have that trivially

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(R_{\Psi}(X)<r(B+1)\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag})\;=\;0\;=\;\mathbb{E}[C_{T}(r)|{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}]\quad\text{ almost surely,}

so it suffices to focus on the case
r\in(0,1]
.

To bound (
27
), we shall provide a finer characterisation of the conditional distribution of
R_{+}
and
R_{-}
given
{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}
and
\lbag V\rbag=\lbag V_{0},\ldots,V_{B}\rbag
. Denote
V^{*}=(V^{*}_{0},\ldots,V^{*}_{B})
, the sequence of ordered statistics. Write
V_{-b}
as the sequence
V
but with the
b
-th element omitted, and
V^{*}_{-b}
as the sequence
V^{*}
but with the
b
-th element omitted. Since
\Psi_{0},\ldots,\Psi_{B}
are exchangeable,
(V_{0},\ldots,V_{B})
are exchangeable given
{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}
and thereby exchangeable given
{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}
and
\lbag V\rbag
. In particular,
(V_{0},\lbag V_{-0}\rbag)\,|\,({\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\lbag V\rbag)
is conditionally uniformly distributed over the size-
(B+1)
list
\{(V^{*}_{0},\lbag V^{*}_{-0}\rbag)\,,\,\ldots\,,\,(V^{*}_{B},\lbag V^{*}_{-B}\rbag)\}
. This implies that

\displaystyle(R_{-},R_{+})\;=\;\big(\msum_{v\in\lbag V\rbag}\mathbb{I}\{V_{0}>v\}\,,\,\msum_{v\in\lbag V\rbag}\mathbb{I}\{V_{0}\geq v\}-1\big)

can be generated by sampling
U\sim\textrm{Uniform}\{0,\ldots,B\}
and setting

\displaystyle R_{-}\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{v\in\lbag V\rbag}\mathbb{I}_{\{V^{*}_{U}>v\}}\;=\;\msum_{b\in[B]_{0}}\mathbb{I}_{\{V^{*}_{U}>V^{*}_{b}\}}\;\overset{\eqref{eq:rank:fn:alt}}{=}\;R^{*}_{-}(U)\;,

\displaystyle R_{+}\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{v\in\lbag V\rbag}\mathbb{I}_{\{V^{*}_{U}\geq v\}}-1\;=\;\msum_{b\in[B]_{0}}\mathbb{I}_{\{V^{*}_{U}\geq V^{*}_{b}\}}-1\;\overset{\eqref{eq:rank:fn:alt}}{=}\;R^{*}_{+}(U)\;.

This gives the following distributional controls on
R_{+}
and
R_{-}
: Almost surely,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big(\,R_{+}<r(B+1)-1\;\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\big)\;=

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(\,R^{*}_{+}(U)<\lceil r(B+1)\rceil-1\;\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\big)

\displaystyle\;\overset{\eqref{eq:rank:fn:property:one}}{=}

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(\,U<R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})\;\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R^*_-(B_r)}{B + 1}$}}\;,

(28)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big(\,R_{-}\geq r(B+1)\;\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\big)\;=

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(\,R^{*}_{-}(U)>\lceil r(B+1)\rceil-1\;\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\big)

\displaystyle\;\overset{\eqref{eq:rank:fn:property:one}}{=}

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(\,U>R^{*}_{+}(B_{r})\;\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B - R^*_+(B_r)}{B + 1}$}}\;.

(29)

We have used that
B_{r}=\lceil r(B+1)\rceil-1
and that
R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})
and
R^{*}_{+}(B_{r})
are both
\lbag V\rbag
-measurable above. Notice that (
28
) gives the expression for the first term of (
27
). To compute the second term of (
27
), note that on the event
R^{*}_{-}(U)<r(B+1)\leq R^{*}_{+}(U)+1
,

\displaystyle\min\{b\in[B]_{0}\;|\;V^{*}_{b}=V^{*}_{U}\}-1\;<\;r(B+1)-1\;\leq\;\max\{b\in[B]_{0}\;|\;V^{*}_{b}=V^{*}_{U}\}

and therefore

\displaystyle\min\{b\in[B]_{0}\;|\;V^{*}_{b}=V^{*}_{U}\}\;\leq\;\lceil r(B+1)\rceil-1\;=\;B_{r}\;\leq\;\max\{b\in[B]_{0}\;|\;V^{*}_{b}=V^{*}_{U}\}\;,

which implies

\displaystyle R^{*}_{-}(U)\;=

\displaystyle\;R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})

and

\displaystyle R^{*}_{+}(U)\;=

\displaystyle\;R^{*}_{+}(B_{r})\;,

both of which are constant almost surely given
\lbag V\rbag
. Therefore, almost surely

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[

\displaystyle\,p_{T}(r(B+1)-R_{-}\,,\,R_{+}-R_{-}+1)\,\mathbb{I}_{\{R_{-}<r(B+1)\leq R_{+}+1\}}\;\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;p_{T}\big(r(B+1)-R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})\,,\,R^{*}_{+}(B_{r})-R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})+1\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\,\times\,{\mathbb{P}}\big(R_{-}<r(B+1)\leq R_{+}+1\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\big)

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;p_{T}\big(r(B+1)-R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})\,,\,R^{*}_{+}(B_{r})-R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})+1\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Big(1-{\mathbb{P}}\big(R_{-}\geq r(B+1)\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\big)-{\mathbb{P}}\big(R_{+}<r(B+1)-1\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\;\overset{(b)}{=}

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\Big(T\big(R^{*}_{+}(B_{r})-R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})+1\big)<r(B+1)-R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})\,\big|\,\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\Big)\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R^*_+(B_r) - R^*_-(B_r) + 1}{B + 1}$}}\;.

In
(a)
, we have noted that
R_{-}\leq R_{+}
almost surely, and in
(b)
, we have used (
28
) and (
29
) as well as the definition of
p_{T}
. Combining this with (
28
) again, almost surely

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(R_{\Psi}(X)<rB\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag})\;=\;\eqref{eq:rank:bound:intermediate}

\displaystyle\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R^*_-(B_r)}{B + 1}$}}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{P}}\Big(T(R^{*}_{+}(B_{r})-R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})+1)<r(B+1)-R^{*}_{-}(B_{r})\,\big|\,\textstyle{\lbag V\rbag}\Big)\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R^*_+(B_r) - R^*_-(B_r) + 1}{B + 1}$}}

\displaystyle\;=\;C_{T}(r)\;,

which proves (
26
) and therefore finishes the proof. ∎

Appendix D
Additional results and proofs for
Section

3.2
: Approximate invariance

D.1. Variants of
Theorem

3

As mentioned at the end of
Section

3.2
,
Theorem

3
can be extended to allow for obtaining conditional coverage on
{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}
, and we can additionally relax the conditioning on
X
in
{\mathbb{P}}(f(\Phi_{1}(X))\leq t|X)
to a general
\sigma
-algebra. In this section, we state a result that includes both extensions. We first state a relaxation of
1
:

Assumption 8
.

(i)
f(\Phi_{1}(X)),\ldots,f(\Phi_{B}(X))
are conditionally i.i.d. and independent of
f(X)
given a
\sigma
-algebra
\mathbb{A}
with
{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\subseteq\mathbb{A}\subseteq\sigma(X)
; (ii) smoothed uniform tie-breaking is used; (iii) the output space of
f
,
\mathcal{Y}
, is taken to be
\mathbb{R}
.

The corresponding measure of approximate invariance is then

\displaystyle\Delta_{\rm inv}(\mathbb{A})\;=\;\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|{\mathbb{P}}(f(\Phi_{1}(X))\leq t\,|\,\mathbb{A})\,-\,{\mathbb{P}}(f(X)\leq t\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})\big|\;.

We also define the conditional
L_{\nu}
norm as

\displaystyle\|{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,}\|_{L_{\nu}|{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}}\;\coloneqq\;(\mathbb{E}[|{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,}|^{\nu}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}])^{1/\nu}\;.

The corresponding relaxation of
Theorem

3
reads:

Theorem 8
.

Let
r\in[0,1]
,
\nu\geq 1
and
B\geq 2
. Under Assumption
8
, almost surely

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Phi(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,-\,r\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{12}{4^{1/(\nu+1)}}$}}\,\big(3\,\|\Delta_{\rm inv}(\mathbb{A})\|_{L_{\nu}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}}\big)^{\frac{\nu}{\nu+1}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B-1}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{3 \log B}}{\sqrt{B-1}}$}}\;.

Theorem

3
follows as an immediate corollary by setting
\mathbb{A}=\sigma(X)
and
{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}=\mathbb{A}_{\rm trivial}
. To prove
Theorem

8
, in view of
Theorem

2
, the starting point is to construct an exchangeable sequence
(f(\Psi_{b}(X)))_{0\leq b\leq B}
, which are then compared to the original sequence
(f(X),f(\Phi_{1}(X)),\ldots,f(\Phi_{B}(X)))
. Our choice in this proof is

\displaystyle(f(\Psi_{b}(X)))_{0\leq b\leq B}\qquad\text{ are conditionally i.i.d.\penalty\ distributed as }\qquad f(X)\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\;.

(30)

Another key element of the proof is the observation that, under
8
(ii), we can write the tie-breaking function in (
) as

\displaystyle T(n)\;=\;Un\qquad\text{ for }\;U\sim\textrm{Uniform}[0,1]\;.

In particular, this allows us to write the normalised rank variable as

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Phi(X)}{B+1}$}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B+1}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}\Big(\mathbb{I}_{\{f(X)>f(\Phi_{b}(X))\}}+U\,\mathbb{I}_{\{f(X)=f(\Phi_{b}(X))\}}\Big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{U}{B+1}$}}\;.

Conditioning on the quantities

\displaystyle U\;,\qquad{\mathbb{P}}(f(X)>f(\Phi_{1}(X))\,|\,\mathbb{A},f(X))\;,\qquad{\mathbb{P}}(f(X)=f(\Phi_{1}(X))\,|\,\mathbb{A},f(X))\;,

this is an empirical average of conditionally i.i.d. random variables supported on three points.
Section

D.2
provides tools that carefully controls the distribution of an empirical average of three-point random variables, and
Section

D.3
employs those tools to prove
Theorem

8
.

D.2. Distribution of sums of three-point variables

The key technical ingredient of the proof is the family of 3-point random variables, parameterised by
p,q,u\in[0,1]
and defined as

\displaystyle V\;\coloneqq\;\begin{cases}1&\text{ with probability }p\;,\\
u&\text{ with probability }q\;,\\
0&\text{ with probability }1-p-q\;.\end{cases}

We write
V_{1},V_{2},\ldots
as i.i.d. copies of
V
, and

\displaystyle\bar{V}_{B}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}\;.

The next lemma concerns the derivatives of the c.d.f. of
\bar{V}_{B}
with respect to
p
and
q
. Throughout the proof, we also denote the multinomial coefficient

\displaystyle\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{B}{m,\, k}$}}\Bigr)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B!}{m! k! (B-m-k)!}$}}\;.

Lemma 9
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{R}
and
B\geq 2
. Then

\displaystyle\partial_{p}\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)\;=

\displaystyle\;-B\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\bar{V}_{B-1}\in\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br - 1}{B-1}$}},\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br}{B-1}$}}\Big)\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\partial_{q}\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)\;=

\displaystyle\;-B\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\bar{V}_{B-1}\in\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br- u }{B-1}$}},\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br}{B-1}$}}\Big)\Big)\;.

Proof of
Section

D.2
.

We first express the c.d.f. as a combinatorial sum:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{m=0}^{B}\msum_{k=0}^{B-m}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{B}{m,k}$}}\Bigr)p^{k}q^{m}(1-p-q)^{B-m-k}\,\mathbb{I}_{\{k+um<Br\}}\;.

Differentiating with respect to
p
, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{p}\,

\displaystyle\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\sum_{m=0}^{B-1}\sum_{k=1}^{B-m}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B!}{m! (k-1)! (B-m-k)!}$}}\,p^{k-1}q^{m}(1-p-q)^{B-m-k}\,\mathbb{I}_{\{k+um<Br\}}

\displaystyle\;-\sum_{m=0}^{B-1}\sum_{k=0}^{B-m-1}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B!}{m! k! (B-m-k-1)!}$}}\,p^{k}q^{m}(1-p-q)^{B-m-k-1}\,\mathbb{I}_{\{k+um<Br\}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\sum_{m=0}^{B-1}\sum_{k=0}^{B-m-1}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B!}{m! k! (B-m-k-1)!}$}}\,p^{k}q^{m}(1-p-q)^{B-m-k-1}\,\mathbb{I}_{\{k+1+um<Br\}}

\displaystyle\;-\sum_{m=0}^{B-1}\sum_{k=0}^{B-m-1}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B!}{m! k! (B-m-k-1)!}$}}\,p^{k}q^{m}(1-p-q)^{B-m-k-1}\,\mathbb{I}_{\{k+um<Br\}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;-\sum_{m=0}^{B-1}\sum_{k=0}^{B-m-1}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B!}{m! k! (B-m-k-1)!}$}}\,p^{k}q^{m}(1-p-q)^{B-m-k-1}\,\mathbb{I}_{\{k+um\in[Br-1,Br)\}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;-B\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\bar{V}_{B-1}\in\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br - 1}{B-1}$}},\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br}{B-1}$}}\Big)\Big)\;.

In
(a)
above, we have shifted the index of
k
by
1
in the first sum.
For the derivative with respect to
q
, we instead express

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{m=0}^{B}\msum_{k=0}^{B-m}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{B}{m,k}$}}\Bigr)q^{k}p^{m}(1-p-q)^{B-m-k}\,\mathbb{I}_{\{uk+m<Br\}}\;.

Then the exact same calculation applies, except that the shift of index in
(a)
causes a shift of
u
in the indicator rather than
1
. This implies

\displaystyle\partial_{q}\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)\;=\;-B\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\bar{V}_{B-1}\in\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br - u}{B-1}$}},\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br}{B-1}$}}\Big)\Big)\;.

∎

The next lemma provides two concentration inequalities for
\bar{V}_{B}
.

Lemma 10
.

Let
r\in[0,1]
. If
r\geq p+uq
, then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\geq r)\;\leq\;e^{-2B\,(r-(p+uq))^{2}}\;.

If instead
r\leq p+uq
, then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\leq r)\;\leq\;e^{-2B\,(r-(p+uq))^{2}}\;.

Proof of
Section

D.2
.

First consider the case
r>p+uq
. By the Chernoff bound and that
u\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\geq r)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\inf\nolimits_{t>0}\,e^{-Brt}\,\mathbb{E}\big[e^{B\bar{V}_{B}t}\big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\inf\nolimits_{t>0}\,e^{-Brt}\,\big(1-p-q+qe^{ut}+pe^{t}\big)^{B}\;.

For
t>0
and
u\in[0,1]
, by the fundamental theorem of calculus, we get that

\displaystyle e^{ut}\;=\;\big(1+(e^{t}-1)\big)^{u}\;=

\displaystyle\;1+u(e^{t}-1)\,\mint_{0}^{1}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(1+\theta(e^t-1))^{1-u}}$}}d\theta

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;1-u+ue^{t}\;.

This yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\geq r)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\inf\nolimits_{t>0}\,e^{-Brt}\,\big(1-p-uq+(p+uq)e^{t}\big)^{B}\;.

Suppose
0<p+uq<r<1
. Since the function
x/(1-x)
is increasing for
x<1
, we have
\frac{p+uq}{1-p-uq}<\frac{r}{1-r}
and therefore

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r(1-p-uq)}{(1-r)(p+uq)}$}}>1\;.

Choosing
t=\log\frac{r(1-p-uq)}{(1-r)(p+uq)}\,>0
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\geq r)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(1-r)(p+uq)}{r(1-p-uq)}$}}\Big)^{Br}\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1-p-uq}{1-r}$}}\Big)^{B}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\Big(B\Big(r\log\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{p+uq}{r}$}}+(1-r)\log\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1-p-uq}{1-r}$}}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;e^{-B\,{\rm D}(r\|p+uq)}\;,

where we have defined the binary relative entropy for
r,p^{\prime}\in[0,1]
as

\displaystyle{\rm D}(r\,\|\,p^{\prime})\;\coloneqq\;r\log\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r}{p'}$}}+(1-r)\log\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1-r}{1-p'}$}}\;\geq\;0\;.

We take
D(r\|0)=+\infty
for
r>0
and
D(1\|p^{\prime})=\log\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}
, following the convention in information theory. In the edge case
0=p+uq<r\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\geq r)\;=\;0\;=\;e^{-B\,{\rm D}(r\|0)}\;,

In the other edge case
0<p+uq<r=1
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\geq r)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\inf\nolimits_{t>0}\,\big((1-p-uq)e^{-t}+(p+uq)\big)^{B}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;(p+uq)^{B}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;e^{-B\log\frac{1}{p+uq}}\;=\;e^{-B\,{\rm D}(1\|p+uq)}\;,

In all cases, this proves that, if
r>p+uq
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\geq r)\;\leq\;e^{-B\,{\rm D}(r\|p+uq)}\;.

Now by Pinsker’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle{\rm D}(r\|p+uq)\;\geq\;2(r-(p+uq))^{2}\;,

which gives the desired inequality that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\geq r)\;\leq\;e^{-2B\,(r-p-uq)^{2}}\;.

For the other case where
r<p+uq
, we use the Chernoff bound again and
u\geq 0
to obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\leq r)\;=

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}(-\bar{V}_{B}\geq-r)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\inf\nolimits_{t>0}\,e^{Brt}\,\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-B\bar{V}_{B}t}\big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\inf\nolimits_{t>0}\,e^{Brt}\,\big(1-p-q+qe^{-ut}+pe^{-t}\big)^{B}\;.

For
t>0
and
u\in[0,1]
, we can again use the fundamental theorem of calculus to obtain

\displaystyle e^{-ut}\;=\;\big(1-(1-e^{-t})\big)^{u}\;=

\displaystyle\;1-u(1-e^{-t})\mint_{0}^{1}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(1-\theta(1-e^{-t}))^{1-u}}$}}d\theta

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;1-u+ue^{-t}\;.

This implies

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\leq r)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\inf\nolimits_{t>0}\,e^{Brt}\,\big(1-p-uq+uqe^{-t}+pe^{-t}\big)^{B}

The rest of the proof proceeds similarly to the case
r<p+uq
: If
0<r<p+uq<1
,
\frac{r}{1-r}<\frac{p+uq}{1-p-uq}
, and choosing
t=\log\frac{(1-r)(p+uq)}{r(1-p-uq)}>0
gives

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\leq r)\;\leq\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(1-r) (p+uq) }{r(1-p-uq)}$}}\Big)^{Br}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1-p-uq}{1-r}$}}\Big)^{B}\;=\;e^{-B\,{\rm D}(r\|p+uq)}\;.

In the edge case where
0\leq r<p+uq=1
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\leq r)\;=\;0\;=\;e^{-B\,{\rm D}(1-r\|0)}\;=\;e^{-B\,{\rm D}(r\|1)}\;,

and in the other edge case where
0=r<p<1
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\leq r)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\inf\nolimits_{t>0}\,\big(1-p-uq+uqe^{-t}+pe^{-t}\big)^{B}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;(1-p-uq)^{B}\;=\;e^{-B\,{\rm D}(1\|1-p-uq)}\;=\;e^{-B\,{\rm D}(0\|p+uq)}\;.

This proves that, if
r<p+uq
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\leq r)\;\leq\;e^{-B\,{\rm D}(r\|p+uq)}\;\leq\;e^{-2B\,(r-p-uq)^{2}}\;,

where we have again used Pinsker’s inequality.

Finally in the case
r=p+uq
, both bounds hold trivially since

\displaystyle\max\{{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\geq r)\,,\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}\leq r)\}\;\leq\;1\;=\;e^{-2B\,(r-p-uq)^{2}}\;.

∎

Combining
Sections

D.2
and
D.2
gives the following control on the derivatives:

Lemma 11
.

Define

\displaystyle\rho_{r}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\max\Big\{\min\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br - 1}{B-1}$}},1\Big\}\,,\,0\Big\}

and

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}_{r}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\max\Big\{\min\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br }{B-1}$}},1\Big\}\,,\,0\Big\}\;.

If
\rho_{r}>p+uq
,

\displaystyle\max\big\{\big|\partial_{p}\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)\big|\,,\,\big|\partial_{q}\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)\big|\big\}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;B\,e^{-2(B-1)\,(p+uq-\rho_{r})^{2}}\;,

and if
\rho^{\prime}_{r}<p+uq
,

\displaystyle\max\big\{\big|\partial_{p}\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)\big|\,,\,\big|\partial_{q}\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)\big|\big\}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;B\,e^{-2(B-1)\,(p+uq-\rho^{\prime}_{r})^{2}}\;.

Proof of
Section

D.2
.

In the case
\rho_{r}>p+uq
, we can apply
Sections

D.2
and
D.2
while noting that
\bar{V}_{B-1}\leq 1
almost surely to obtain

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{p}\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)\big|\;=

\displaystyle\;B\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\bar{V}_{B-1}\in\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br - 1}{B-1}$}},\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br}{B-1}$}}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;B\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\bar{V}_{B-1}\geq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br - 1}{B-1}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;B\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\bar{V}_{B-1}\geq\max\Big\{\min\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br - 1}{B-1}$}},1\Big\}\,,\,0\Big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;B\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B-1}\geq\rho_{r})

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;B\,e^{-2(B-1)\,(p+uq-\rho_{r})^{2}}\;.

Moreover since
u\leq 1
, we also have

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{q}\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)\big|\;=

\displaystyle\;B\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\bar{V}_{B-1}\in\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br- u }{B-1}$}},\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br}{B-1}$}}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\big|\partial_{p}\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)\big|\;\leq\;B\,e^{-2(B-1)\,(p+uq-\rho_{r})^{2}}\;.

This proves the first bound. In the case
\rho^{\prime}_{r}<p+uq
, a similar argument gives

\displaystyle\max\big\{\big|\partial_{p}\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)\big|\,,\,\big|\partial_{q}\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B}<r)\big|\big\}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;B\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\bar{V}_{B-1}\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br}{B-1}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;B\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\bar{V}_{B-1}\leq\max\Big\{\min\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br}{B-1}$}},1\Big\}\,,\,0\Big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;B\,{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{V}_{B-1}\leq\rho^{\prime}_{r})

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;B\,e^{-2(B-1)\,(p+uq-\rho^{\prime}_{r})^{2}}\;.

∎

D.3. Proof of
Theorem

8

Step 1: Setup.

We first express the quantities of interest in terms of those defined in
Section

D.2
. We are concerned with the normalised rank variable

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Phi(X)}{B+1}$}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B+1}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}\big(\mathbb{I}_{\{f(X)>f(\Phi_{b}(X))\}}+U\,\mathbb{I}_{\{f(X)=f(\Phi_{b}(X))\}}\big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{U}{B+1}$}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B+1}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{1},Q_{1},U)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{U}{B+1}$}}\;,

where we have defined the random variables
V_{b}(p,q,u)
as in
Section

D.2
, i.e.

\displaystyle V_{b}(p,q,u)\,|\,p,q,u\;\overset{\rm i.i.d.}{\sim}\;\begin{cases}1&\text{ with probability }p\;,\\
u&\text{ with probability }q\;,\\
0&\text{ with probability }1-p-q\;,\end{cases}

and also defined

\displaystyle P_{1}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}(f(X)>f(\Phi_{1}(X))\,|\,\mathbb{A},\,f(X))

and

\displaystyle Q_{1}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}(f(X)=f(\Phi_{1}(X))\,|\,\mathbb{A},\,f(X))\;.

V_{b}(P_{1},Q_{1},U)
’s are conditionally i.i.d. given
P_{1}
,
Q_{1}
and
U
.
Since
\mathbb{A}\supseteq{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}
, by the tower rule,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Phi(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\;=\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{1},Q_{1},U)<r_{U}\,\Big|\,P_{1},Q_{1},U\Big)\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]\;,

where we have denoted

\displaystyle r_{U}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B+1}{B}$}}r-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{U}{B}$}}\;.

Conditioning on
P_{1}
,
Q_{1}
and
U
, we can identify the average above with
\bar{V}_{B}
in
Section

D.2
, where
p,q,u
are replaced by
P_{1}
,
Q_{1}
and
U
respectively.

On the other hand, recall from (
30
) that
(f(\Psi_{b}(X)))_{0\leq b\leq B}
are conditionally i.i.d. copies of
f(X)
given
{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}
. We can WLOG couple
(f(\Psi_{b}(X)))_{0\leq b\leq B}
and
f(X)
such that

\displaystyle f(\Psi_{0}(X))=f(X)\quad\text{ almost surely. }

This allows us to express, as before,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Psi(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\;=\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{2},Q_{2},U)<r_{U}\,\Big|\,P_{2},Q_{2},U\Big)\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]\;,

where we have defined

\displaystyle P_{2}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}(f(X)>f(\Psi_{1}(X))\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\,f(X))\;,

\displaystyle Q_{2}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}(f(X)=f(\Psi_{1}(X))\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\,f(X))\;.

Step 2: Taylor expansion and apply derivative bounds from
Section

D.2
.
Let
\Theta\sim\textrm{Uniform}[0,1]
be independent of all other random variables, and denote the interpolation

\displaystyle P_{\Theta}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\Theta P_{1}+(1-\Theta)P_{2}

and

\displaystyle Q_{\Theta}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\Theta Q_{1}+(1-\Theta)Q_{2}\;.

We will also be applying
Section

D.2
. To this end, recall the notation

\displaystyle\rho_{r_{U}}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\max\Big\{\min\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br_U - 1}{B-1}$}},1\Big\}\,,\,0\Big\}

and

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}_{r_{U}}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\max\Big\{\min\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Br_U}{B-1}$}},1\Big\}\,,\,0\Big\}\;.

(31)

Taking a difference of the c.d.f. above and applying the mean value theorem, we obtain that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Phi(X)}{B+1}$}}\,

\displaystyle\,<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,-\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Psi(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{1},Q_{1},U)<r_{U}\,\Big|\,P_{1},Q_{1},U\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{2},Q_{2},U)<r_{U}\,\Big|\,P_{2},Q_{2},U\Big)\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\,(P_{1}-P_{2})\,\partial_{p}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{\Theta},Q_{\Theta},U)<r_{U}\,\Big|\,P_{\Theta},Q_{\Theta},U\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+(Q_{1}-Q_{2})\,\partial_{q}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{\Theta},Q_{\Theta},U)<r_{U}\,\Big|\,P_{\Theta},Q_{\Theta},U\Big)\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]

\displaystyle\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[(\star)\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}]\;.

Let
\delta>0
. We first write

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[(\star)\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}]|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[|(\star)|\,\big(\mathbb{I}_{\{|P_{1}-P_{2}|\geq\delta\}}+\mathbb{I}_{\{|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|\geq\delta\}}+\mathbb{I}_{\{|P_{1}-P_{2}|<\delta\,,\,|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|<\delta\}}\big)\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big]\;.

Note that
(\star)
is a difference between two quantities taking values in
[0,1]
, and therefore
|(\star)|\leq 1
almost surely. By the Markov inequality, we can bound, for
\nu\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|(\star)|\,\mathbb{I}_{\{|P_{1}-P_{2}|\geq\delta\}}\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(|P_{1}-P_{2}|\geq\delta\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big)\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean[ |P_1 - P_2|^\nu\,|\, \Xobs]}{\delta^\nu}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|(\star)|\,\mathbb{I}_{\{|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|\geq\delta\}}\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|\geq\delta\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big)\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean[ |Q_1 - Q_2|^\nu\,|\, \Xobs]}{\delta^\nu}$}}\;,

(32)

and note also that, for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[|(\star)|\,\mathbb{I}_{\big\{\rho_{r_{U}}-\epsilon\leq P_{\Theta}+UQ_{\Theta}\leq\rho^{\prime}_{r_{U}}+\epsilon\,,\,|P_{1}-P_{2}|<\delta\,,\,|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|<\delta\big\}}\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]

\displaystyle\;\leq\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(\rho_{r_{U}}-\epsilon-2\delta\leq P_{2}+UQ_{2}\leq\rho^{\prime}_{r_{U}}+\epsilon+2\delta\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big)\;.

This allows us to write

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[(\star)\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}]

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean[ |P_1 - P_2|^\nu\,|\, \Xobs]}{\delta^\nu}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean[ |Q_1 - Q_2|^\nu\,|\, \Xobs]}{\delta^\nu}$}}

\displaystyle\;\qquad+{\mathbb{P}}\big(\rho_{r_{U}}-\epsilon-2\delta\leq P_{2}+UQ_{2}\leq\rho^{\prime}_{r_{U}}+\epsilon+2\delta\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big)

\displaystyle\;\qquad+\mathbb{E}\Big[|(\star)|\,\mathbb{I}_{\{|P_{1}-P_{2}|<\delta\,,\,|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|<\delta\}}\Big(\mathbb{I}_{\{\rho_{r_{U}}-\epsilon>P_{\Theta}+UQ_{\Theta}\}}+\mathbb{I}_{\{\rho^{\prime}_{r_{U}}+\epsilon<P_{\Theta}+UQ_{\Theta}\}}\Big)\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]\;.

To handle the last term, we write as shorthand

\displaystyle M\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\max\Big\{\Big|\partial_{p}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{\Theta},Q_{\Theta},U)<r_{U}\,\Big|\,P_{\Theta},Q_{\Theta},U\Big)\Big|\,,\,

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\Big|\partial_{q}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{\Theta},Q_{\Theta},U)<r_{U}\,\Big|\,P_{\Theta},Q_{\Theta},U\Big)\Big|\Big\}\;.

This allows us to write

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[|(\star)|\,\mathbb{I}_{\{|P_{1}-P_{2}|<\delta\,,\,|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|<\delta\}}\Big(\mathbb{I}_{\{\rho_{r_{U}}-\epsilon>P_{\Theta}+UQ_{\Theta}\}}+\mathbb{I}_{\{\rho^{\prime}_{r_{U}}+\epsilon<P_{\Theta}+UQ_{\Theta}\}}\Big)\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\mathbb{E}\Big[(|P_{1}-P_{2}|+|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|)\,M\,\mathbb{I}_{\{|P_{1}-P_{2}|<\delta\,,\,|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|<\delta\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\Big(\mathbb{I}_{\{\rho_{r_{U}}-\epsilon>P_{\Theta}+UQ_{\Theta}\}}+\mathbb{I}_{\{\rho^{\prime}_{r_{U}}+\epsilon<P_{\Theta}+UQ_{\Theta}\}}\Big)\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]

\displaystyle\;\leq\;2\delta\,\mathbb{E}\Big[M\Big(\mathbb{I}_{\{\rho_{r_{U}}-\epsilon>P_{\Theta}+UQ_{\Theta}\}}+\mathbb{I}_{\{\rho^{\prime}_{r_{U}}+\epsilon<P_{\Theta}+UQ_{\Theta}\}}\Big)\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]

\displaystyle\;\leq\;4\delta Be^{-2(B-1)\epsilon^{2}}\;,

where, in the last line, we have recalled that
P_{\Theta}=\Theta(P_{1}-P_{2})+P_{2}
and
Q_{\Theta}=\Theta(Q_{1}-Q_{2})+Q_{2}
, and applied the derivative bounds in
Section

D.2
on
M
. Combining all the bounds above, we obtain

\displaystyle\;\Big|{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Phi(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,-\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Psi(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean[ |P_1 - P_2|^\nu\,|\, \Xobs]}{\delta^\nu}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean[ |Q_1 - Q_2|^\nu\,|\, \Xobs]}{\delta^\nu}$}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+{\mathbb{P}}\big(\rho_{r_{U}}-\epsilon-2\delta\leq P_{2}+UQ_{2}\leq\rho^{\prime}_{r_{U}}+\epsilon+2\delta\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big)+4B\delta e^{-2(B-1)\epsilon^{2}}\;.

(33)

Step 3: Use uniformity of
P_{2}+UQ_{2}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}
.
To control the remaining terms, we first recall that

\displaystyle P_{2}+UQ_{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(f(\Psi_{1}(X))<f(X)\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},f(X)\big)+U\,{\mathbb{P}}\big(f(\Psi_{1}(X))=f(X)\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\,f(X)\big)\;,

where
f(\Psi_{1}(X))
is a conditionally i.i.d. copy of
f(X)
given
{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}
. We seek to prove that
P_{2}+UQ_{2}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}
is uniformly distributed. To see this, for
u\in[0,1]
, define

\displaystyle y_{u}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\inf\big\{y\in\mathbb{R}\,\big|\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(f(\Psi_{1}(X))\leq y\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big)\geq u\big\}\;.

Then by definition,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big(f(\Psi_{1}(X))<y_{u}\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big)\;<\;u\;\leq\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(f(\Psi_{1}(X))\leq y_{u}\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big)\;.

Consider three cases depending on the value of
f(X)
:

\bullet

On the event
\{f(X)<y_{u}\}
, almost surely

\displaystyle P_{2}+Q_{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(f(\Psi_{1}(X))\leq f(X)\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\,f(X)\big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(f(\Psi_{1}(X))<y_{u}\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\,f(X)\big)\;\leq\;u\;;

\bullet

On the event
\{f(X)>y_{u}\}
, almost surely

\displaystyle P_{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(f(\Psi_{1}(X))<f(X)\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\,f(X)\big)

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}\big(f(\Psi_{1}(X))\leq y_{u}\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},\,f(X)\big)\;\geq\;u\;;

\bullet

On the event
\{f(X)=y_{u}\}
, almost surely

\displaystyle P_{2}+UQ_{2}\;\leq\;u\quad\text{ if and only if }\quad U\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{u- \P( f(\Psi_1(X)) < y_u | \Xobs)}{\P( f(\Psi_1(X)) = y_u | \Xobs)}$}}\;.

This implies that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(P_{2}+UQ_{2}\leq u\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})\;=

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}(f(X)<y_{u}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})

\displaystyle\;+{\mathbb{P}}\Big(f(X)=y_{u}\,,\,U\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{u- \P( f(\Psi_1(X)) < y_u | \Xobs)}{\P( f(\Psi_1(X)) = y_u | \Xobs)}$}}\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\;.

Since
U\sim\textrm{Uniform}[0,1]
is independent of
f(X)
, we can compute

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(P_{2}+UQ_{2}\leq u\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})\;=

\displaystyle\;{\mathbb{P}}(f(X)<y_{u}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})

\displaystyle\;+{\mathbb{P}}(f(X)=y_{u}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{u- \P( f(\Psi_1(X)) < y_u | \Xobs)}{\P( f(\Psi_1(X)) = y_u | \Xobs)}$}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;u\;.

Since this holds for all
u
, we obtain that
P_{2}+UQ_{2}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}
is distributed as
\textrm{Uniform}[0,1]
. Plugging this into (
33
), and noting that
0\leq\rho^{\prime}_{r_{U}}-\rho_{r_{U}}\leq\frac{1}{B-1}
by their definitions in (
31
), we obtain

\displaystyle\;\Big|{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Phi(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,-\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Psi(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean[ |P_1 - P_2|^\nu\,|\, \Xobs]}{\delta^\nu}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean[ |Q_1 - Q_2|^\nu\,|\, \Xobs]}{\delta^\nu}$}}+\big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B-1}$}}+2\epsilon+4\delta\big)+4B\delta e^{-2(B-1)\epsilon^{2}}\;.

(34)

Step 4: Extract approximate invariance term.
We now control
|P_{1}-P_{2}|
and
|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|
. Recall

\displaystyle\Delta_{\rm inv}(\mathbb{A})\;=\;\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|{\mathbb{P}}(f(\Phi_{1}(X))\leq t\,|\,\mathbb{A})\,-\,{\mathbb{P}}(f(X)\leq t\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})\big|\;.

This allows us to bound

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[|P_{1}-P_{2}|^{\nu}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}]\;=\;\mathbb{E}\big[\;\big|\,{\mathbb{P}}(f(\Phi_{1}(X))<f(X)\,|\,\mathbb{A},f(X))

\displaystyle\;\hskip 120.00018pt-\;{\mathbb{P}}(f(\Psi_{1}(X))<f(X)\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},f(X))\,\big|^{\nu}\;\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\mathbb{E}\big[\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|{\mathbb{P}}(f(\Phi_{1}(X))\leq t\,|\,\mathbb{A})\,-\,{\mathbb{P}}(f(\Psi_{1}(X))\leq t\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})\big|^{\nu}\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big]

\displaystyle\;=\;\big\|\Delta_{\rm inv}(\mathbb{A})\big\|_{L_{\nu}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}}^{\nu}\;,

(35)

where we have used that
f(\Psi_{1}(X))
is a conditionally i.i.d. copy of
f(X)
given
{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|^{\nu}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}]\;=\;\mathbb{E}\big[\;\big|\,{\mathbb{P}}(f(\Phi_{1}(X))=f(X)\,|\,\mathbb{A},f(X))

\displaystyle\;\hskip 120.00018pt-\;{\mathbb{P}}(f(\Psi_{1}(X))=f(X)\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}},f(X))\,\big|^{\nu}\;\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq\;2^{\nu}\,\mathbb{E}\big[\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|{\mathbb{P}}(f(\Phi_{1}(X))\leq t\,|\,\mathbb{A})\,-\,{\mathbb{P}}(f(\Psi_{1}(X))\leq t\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}})\big|^{\nu}\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big]

\displaystyle\;=\;2^{\nu}\,\big\|\Delta_{\rm inv}(\mathbb{A})\big\|_{L_{\nu}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}}^{\nu}\;.

Plugging these into (
34
) gives

\displaystyle\;\Big|{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Phi(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,-\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Psi(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3^\nu\, \| \Delta_{\rm inv}(\A) \|_{L_\nu\,|\, \Xobs}^\nu}{\delta^\nu}$}}+\big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B-1}$}}+2\epsilon+4\delta\big)+4B\delta e^{-2(B-1)\epsilon^{2}}\;.

Step 5: Clean-up.
The bound above holds for any
\epsilon,\delta>0
. Choosing

\displaystyle\delta\;=\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ (3 \, \| \Delta_{\rm inv}(\A) \|_{L_\nu\,|\, \Xobs})^\nu}{4}$}}\Big)^{1/(\nu+1)}\;,\quad\epsilon\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{3 \log B}}{ 2 \,\sqrt{B-1}}$}}\;,

(36)

we obtain

\displaystyle\;\Big|{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Phi(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\,-\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_\Psi(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq\;4\delta+\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B-1}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{3 \log B}}{\sqrt{B-1}}$}}+4\delta\Big)+4\delta\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{B}}$}}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;12\delta+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B-1}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{3 \log B}}{\sqrt{B-1}}$}}

\displaystyle\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{12}{4^{1/(\nu+1)}}$}}\,\big(3\,\|\Delta_{\rm inv}(\mathbb{A})\|_{L_{\nu}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}}\big)^{\frac{\nu}{\nu+1}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B-1}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{3 \log B}}{\sqrt{B-1}}$}}\;.

The desired bound is obtained by noting that, by construction,
(f(\Psi_{b}(X)))_{0\leq b\leq B}
is a conditionally exchangeable sequence given
{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}
, so by
Theorem

2
with
Remark

2
, we have
{\mathbb{P}}\big(\frac{R_{\Psi}(X)}{B+1}<r\,\big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\big)=r
.
∎

D.4. Proof of
Section

3.2

We prove instead a more general version of
Section

3.2
, which allows for conditioning on
{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}
:

Lemma 12
.

Let
r\in[0,1]
,
\nu\geq 1
and
(\Phi^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\Phi^{\prime}_{B})
be a deterministic enumeration of a size-
B
set
\mathcal{S}_{B}
. Let
(\Phi_{b})_{b\leq B}
be i.i.d. uniform draws from the same set
\mathcal{S}_{B}
independent of all other variables. Under
1
(ii) and (iii),

\displaystyle\;\Big|{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Phi'}(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)-{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Phi}(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 \sqrt{\log(2B)}}{\sqrt{2B}}$}}\,+\,5\,\big(3\big\|\Delta_{\rm inv}(X)\big\|_{L_{\nu}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}}\big)^{\nu/(\nu+1)}\;,

where we have denoted
R_{\Phi^{\prime}}(X)\coloneqq{\rm Rank}_{T}\big(f(X)\,;\,(f(\Phi^{\prime}_{b}(X)))_{b\in[B]}\big)
.

Proof of
Section

D.4
.

Since
\Phi^{\prime}=(\Phi^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\Phi^{\prime}_{B})
is a deterministic enumeration of
\mathcal{S}_{B}
and
\Phi_{b}
’s are i.i.d. drawn from
\mathcal{S}_{B}
, we can express

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Phi'}(X)}{B+1}$}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B}{B+1}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\,\msum_{b=1}^{B}\mathbb{I}_{\{f(X)>f(\Phi^{\prime}_{b}(X))\}}+U\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\,\msum_{b=1}^{B}\mathbb{I}_{\{f(X)=f(\Phi^{\prime}_{b}(X))\}}\Big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{U}{B+1}$}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B}{B+1}$}}\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{I}_{\{f(X)>f(\Phi_{b}(X))\}}\,|\,X\big]+U\,\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{I}_{\{f(X)=f(\Phi_{b}(X))\}}\,|\,X\big]\Big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{U}{B+1}$}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B}{B+1}$}}(P_{1}+U\,Q_{1})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{U}{B+1}$}}\;,

where we have inherited the notation
P_{1}
and
Q_{1}
from the proof of
Theorem

8
in
Section

D.3
with
\mathbb{A}=\sigma(X)
. We also inherit the notation
V_{b}
,
P_{2}
and
Q_{2}
from
Section

D.3
, and denote

\displaystyle r_{U}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B+1}{B}$}}r-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{U}{B}$}}\;.

This allows us to write

\displaystyle(*)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\Big|{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Phi'}(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)-{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R_{\Phi}(X)}{B+1}$}}<r\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{I}_{\{P_{1}+U\,Q_{1}<r_{U}\}}-\mathbb{I}_{\{\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{1},Q_{1},U)<r_{U}\}}\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{I}_{\{P_{1}+U\,Q_{1}<r_{U}\,,\,\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{1},Q_{1},U)\geq r_{U}\}}-\mathbb{I}_{\{P_{1}+U\,Q_{1}\geq r_{U}\,,\,\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{1},Q_{1},U)<r_{U}\}}\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]\Big|\;.

As with
Section

D.3
, we let
\delta>0
, apply the Markov’s inequality (
32
) and the bound (
35
) with
\nu\geq 1
to obtain

\displaystyle(*)\;\leq\;\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{I}_{\{|P_{1}-P_{2}|<\delta\,,\,|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|<\delta\}}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt\Big(\mathbb{I}_{\{P_{1}+U\,Q_{1}<r_{U}\,,\,\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{1},Q_{1},U)\geq r_{U}\}}-\mathbb{I}_{\{P_{1}+U\,Q_{1}\geq r_{U}\,,\,\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{1},Q_{1},U)<r_{U}\}}\Big)\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3 \, \| \Delta_{\rm inv}(X) \|_{L_\nu\,|\, \Xobs}^\nu}{\delta^\nu}$}}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{I}_{\{|P_{1}-P_{2}|<\delta\,,\,|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|<\delta\,,\,P_{1}+U\,Q_{1}<r_{U}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\,\times\,\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{1},Q_{1},U)\geq r_{U}\,\Big|\,P_{1},Q_{1},U\Big)\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{I}_{\{|P_{1}-P_{2}|<\delta\,,\,|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|<\delta\,,\,P_{1}+U\,Q_{1}\geq r_{U}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\,\times\,\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{1},Q_{1},U)<r_{U}\,\Big|\,P_{1},Q_{1},U\Big)\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3^\nu\, \| \Delta_{\rm inv}(X) \|_{L_\nu\,|\, \Xobs}^\nu}{\delta^\nu}$}}\;.

By the concentration bounds in
Section

D.2
, we have that almost surely

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{I}_{\{P_{1}+U\,Q_{1}<r_{U}\}}\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{1},Q_{1},U)\geq r_{U}\,\Big|\,P_{1},Q_{1},U\Big)\;\leq\;e^{-2B\,(r_{U}-(P_{1}+UQ_{1}))^{2}}\;,

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{I}_{\{P_{1}+U\,Q_{1}\geq r_{U}\}}\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}\msum_{b=1}^{B}V_{b}(P_{1},Q_{1},U)<r_{U}\,\Big|\,P_{1},Q_{1},U\Big)\;\leq\;e^{-2B\,(r_{U}-(P_{1}+UQ_{1}))^{2}}\;,

which implies

\displaystyle(*)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;2\,\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{I}_{\{|P_{1}-P_{2}|<\delta\,,\,|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|<\delta\}}\,e^{-2B\,(r_{U}-(P_{1}+UQ_{1}))^{2}}\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big]\Big|+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3^\nu\, \| \Delta_{\rm inv}(X) \|_{L_\nu\,|\, \Xobs}^\nu}{\delta^\nu}$}}\;.

Let
\epsilon>0
. We can further split the expectation above according to how
|r_{U}-(P_{1}+UQ_{1})|
compares with
\epsilon
, and obtain

\displaystyle(*)\;\leq\;2\,e^{-2B\epsilon^{2}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3^\nu\, \| \Delta_{\rm inv}(X) \|_{L_\nu\,|\, \Xobs}^\nu}{\delta^\nu}$}}

\displaystyle\;\qquad\quad+2\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(|P_{1}-P_{2}|<\delta\,,\,|Q_{1}-Q_{2}|<\delta\,,\,|P_{1}+UQ_{1}-r_{U}|<\epsilon\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq\;2\,e^{-2B\epsilon^{2}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3^\nu\, \| \Delta_{\rm inv}(X) \|_{L_\nu\,|\, \Xobs}^\nu}{\delta^\nu}$}}+{\mathbb{P}}\Big(P_{2}+UQ_{2}\in(r_{U}-\epsilon-2\delta,r_{U}+\epsilon+2\delta)\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=\;2\,e^{-2B\epsilon^{2}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3^\nu\, \| \Delta_{\rm inv}(X) \|_{L_\nu\,|\, \Xobs}^\nu}{\delta^\nu}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B}$}}+2\epsilon+4\delta\;.

In the last line, we have recalled from (
34
) that
P_{2}+UQ_{2}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}\sim\textrm{Uniform}[0,1]
and that
r_{U}-\frac{B+1}{B}r\in[-\frac{1}{B},0]
almost surely. Choosing

\displaystyle\epsilon\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{\log(2B)}}{\sqrt{2B}}$}}

and

\displaystyle\delta\;=

\displaystyle\;\big(3\big\|\Delta_{\rm inv}(X)\big\|_{L_{\nu}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}}\big)^{\nu/(\nu+1)}\;,

we obtain the desired bound that

\displaystyle(*)\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 \sqrt{\log(2B)}}{\sqrt{2B}}$}}\,+\,5\,\big(3\big\|\Delta_{\rm inv}(X)\big\|_{L_{\nu}\,|\,{\mathbb{X}_{\rm obs}}}\big)^{\nu/(\nu+1)}\;.

∎

Appendix E
Additional results for
Section

3.3
: Coverage under universality

The first goal of this section is to prove a generalisation of
Theorem

5
, which provides an explicit bound on the quantity

\displaystyle\Delta_{\rm inv}(X)\;=\;\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|{\mathbb{P}}(f(\Phi_{1}(X))\leq t\,|\,X)\,-\,{\mathbb{P}}(f(X)\leq t)\big|\;.

This bound, provided in
Theorem

16
, relies on a conditional universality result for stable functions of locally dependent data (
Theorem

14
). We state, interpret and prove these results in
Sections

E.1
and
E.2
.

The second goal of this section is to verify the bound on
\Delta_{\rm inv}(X)
for the examples considered in
Section

3.3
. This consists of two parts: In
Section

E.3
, we compute the local dependence measure for different transformations
(\Phi_{b})_{b\leq B}
; in
Section

E.4
, we verify the stability conditions for different estimators
f
. We note that for a restricted range of transformations that operate globally on the data set e.g. those used in permutation tests and conformal prediction, we can still verify local dependence and stability by a suitable reformulation of the estimators, and details are in
Sections

E.3
and
E.4
. For an assessment of the moment approximation quality of selected transformations, we refer readers to the specific examples in
Appendix

G
.

E.1. Gaussian universality under local dependence

This section presents a generic Gaussian universality result for
\mathbb{R}^{m}
-valued random vectors under local dependence and for a thrice-differentiable function
g:\mathbb{R}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
. Let

\displaystyle V\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;(V_{1},\ldots,V_{m})

and

\displaystyle W\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;(W_{1},\ldots,W_{m})

be two
\mathbb{R}^{m}
-valued random vectors, satisfying that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[V]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[W]\;=\;0\;

and

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[W]\;=

\displaystyle\;I_{m}\;.

The Gaussian surrogate
\xi=(\xi_{i})_{i\leq m}
, chosen independently of all other variables, is distributed as

\displaystyle\xi\;\sim

\displaystyle\;\mathcal{N}(0,I_{m})\;.

For
\text{\rm Var}[V]
sufficiently close to
I_{m}
, we seek to establish a universality approximation of the form

\displaystyle g(V)\;\approx\;g(\xi)\;\approx\;g(W)\;,

where the approximation of the random variables holds with respect to weak convergence as
m
becomes large. To formalise this, we need to first formalise the notion of local dependence, stability and anti-concentration.

Local dependence.
For
1\leq i\leq m
, the local dependency neighbourhoods of
V_{i}
in
V
and
W_{i}
in
W
are respectively

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{i}^{V}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\inf\big\{\mathcal{M}\subseteq[m]\;\big|\;i\in\mathcal{M}\text{ and }(V_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{M}}\text{ is independent of }(V_{j})_{j\not\in\mathcal{M}}\big\}\;,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{i}^{W}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\inf\big\{\mathcal{M}\subseteq[m]\;\big|\;i\in\mathcal{M}\text{ and }(W_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{M}}\text{ is independent of }(W_{j})_{j\not\in\mathcal{M}}\big\}\;.

Note that, writing
\mu_{V}
and
\mu_{W}
as the probability measures of
V
and
W
,
N({\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,})
defined in (
12
) satisfies

\displaystyle N(\mu_{V})\;=

\displaystyle\;\max\nolimits_{1\leq i\leq m}\big|\mathcal{M}_{i}^{V}\big|

and

\displaystyle N(\mu_{W})\;=

\displaystyle\;\max\nolimits_{1\leq i\leq m}\big|\mathcal{M}_{i}^{W}\big|\;.

We shall measure the maximum local dependency as

\displaystyle N\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\max\nolimits_{1\leq i\leq m}\max\big\{\big|\mathcal{M}_{i}^{V}\big|\,,\,\big|\mathcal{M}_{i}^{W}\big|\big\}\;.

V
I^{(1)}(s,0)
I^{(1)}(s,\theta)
\cdots
I^{(m)}(s,\theta)
I^{(m)}(s,0)
I(s)
\xi

Figure 8
:

Illustration of the interpolation paths between the two random vectors
V
and
\xi
. The
blue
solid path varies
s\in[0,1]
to interpolate between
V_{i}
and
\xi_{i}
. Each
red
dashed path, indexed by
I^{(i)}(s,\theta)
, varies
\theta\in[0,1]
to interpolate between the set of variables inside the dependency neighbourhood of the
i
-th coordinate and a set of zeros. The same interpolation paths are used between
W
and
\xi
, as well as the corresponding
\mathbb{R}^{nd}
random variables in
Section

3.3
.

Stability.
Let
g:\mathbb{R}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a thrice-differentiable statistic of interest. As with the convention in the universality literature, the result will be stated in terms of the stability of
g
along a suitably chosen interpolation path. Our choice of interpolation path is slightly more complicated due to the local dependence structure, and follows a similar recipe as
27
in the case of high-dimensional logistic regression. For
s\in[0,1]
, we first consider the interpolation path between
V
and
\xi
,

\displaystyle I(s)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;(I_{i}(s))_{i\leq m}\;,

\displaystyle\text{ where }\quad I_{i}(s)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\sqrt{s}\;V_{i}\,+\,\sqrt{1-s}\;\xi_{i}\;.

For
i\leq m
, we also consider an interpolation path between
I(s)
and a variant of
I(s)
with the dependency neighbourhood
\mathcal{M}^{V}_{i}
removed: For
\theta\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\;I^{(i)}_{j}(s,\theta)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\theta I_{j}(s)&\text{ if }j\in\mathcal{M}_{i}^{V}\\
I_{j}(s)&\text{ if }j\not\in\mathcal{M}_{i}^{V}\end{cases}\;\text{ for }1\leq j\leq m\;,\quad I^{(i)}(s,\theta)\coloneqq\Big(I^{(i)}_{j}(s,\theta)\Big)_{j\leq m}\;.

We also define the analogous interpolation quantities for
W
:

\displaystyle J(s)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;(J_{i}(s))_{i\leq m}\;,

\displaystyle\text{ where }\quad J_{i}(s)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\sqrt{s}\;W_{i}\,+\,\sqrt{1-s}\;\xi_{i}\;,

and

\displaystyle\;J^{(i)}_{j}(s,\theta)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\theta J_{j}(s)&\text{ if }j\in\mathcal{M}_{i}^{W}\\
J_{j}(s)&\text{ if }j\not\in\mathcal{M}_{i}^{W}\end{cases}\;\text{ for }1\leq j\leq m\;,\quad J^{(i)}(s,\theta)\coloneqq\Big(J^{(i)}_{j}(s,\theta)\Big)_{j\leq m}\;.

Figure
8
includes a pictorial illustration of our choice of our interpolation paths. We measure the stability of
g
by its first three partial derivatives along these interpolation paths: For
r=1,2,3
,

\displaystyle\varphi_{r}(g)\;\coloneqq\;m^{r/2}\hskip-5.0pt\sup_{i,j\leq m;\,\theta,s\in[0,1]}\hskip-5.0pt\,\max\big\{\,\big\|\,\partial_{j}^{r}\,g\big(I^{(i)}(s,\theta)\big)\,\big\|_{L_{4}}\,,\,\big\|\,\partial_{j}^{r}\,g\big(J^{(i)}(s,\theta)\big)\,\big\|_{L_{4}}\,\big\}\;.

Notice that the stability terms
\varphi_{r;\nu}
defined in (
13
) in
Section

3.3
are exactly the analogues of these quantities with an additional
\|{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,}\|_{L_{\nu}}
. We make several remarks about these notions of stability:

\bullet

\varphi_{r}(g)
can be replaced by uniform upper bounds on the partial derivatives of
g
. We state them as location-dependent bounds on the interpolation paths, which can often lead to tighter controls in specific examples; see the next bullet point and e.g.
65
.

\bullet

The normalisation in
\varphi_{r}(g)
is chosen in view of the following toy example. If
g(V)=(\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\sum_{i\leq m}V_{i})^{\kappa}
, i.e. some integer power of the empirical average with
\kappa\geq 3
, and
V_{i}
’s are i.i.d.
\mathbb{R}
-valued, zero-mean and bounded, we have that for all
1\leq i\leq m
,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{i}\,g(V)\|_{L_{4}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big\|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\kappa}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\msum_{i\leq m}V_{i}\Big)^{\kappa-1}\Big\|_{L_{4}}\;=\;O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\|\partial^{2}_{i}\,g(V)\|_{L_{4}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big\|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\kappa(\kappa-1)}{m}$}}\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\msum_{i\leq m}V_{i}\Big)^{\kappa-2}\Big\|_{L_{4}}\;=\;O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{m}$}}\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\|\partial^{3}_{i}\,g(V)\|_{L_{4}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big\|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\kappa(\kappa-1)(\kappa-2)}{m^{3/2}}$}}\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\msum_{i\leq m}V_{i}\Big)^{\kappa-3}\Big\|_{L_{4}}\;=\;O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{m^{3/2}}$}}\Big)\;.

Applying a pre-multiplier of
m^{r/2}
on each
r
-th derivative therefore ensures that the derivative terms are
O(1)
. This is in general the right scaling for
g
’s that can be well-approximated by a Taylor expansion, have a non-degenerate limit, and are exchangeable in their inputs; see e.g.
45
for a formal treatment for symmetric U-statistics, V-statistics and a higher-order delta method.

\bullet

While not considered in this article, we remark that the stability of an estimator can be improved by techniques such as bootstrap aggregation (bagging)
14
,
81
.

Smooth functions and anti-concentration.
As part of the proof, we shall approximate the Kolmogorov distance

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|{\mathbb{P}}(g(V)\leq t)-{\mathbb{P}}(g(W)\leq t)|

(37)

by an integral probability distance of the form

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|\mathbb{E}[h_{t}(g(V))-h_{t}(g(W))]|\;,

(38)

where
(h_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
is a suitably chosen class of functions with a prescribed level of smoothness; see a standard usage in the literature for distributional approximation e.g. the proof of Theorem 3.3 in
[
13
]
. For example, to obtain an
1
-Lipschitz approximation, one may consider

h_{1;t;\delta}(x)\;\coloneqq\;\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }x<t-\delta\;,\\
\frac{t-x}{\delta}&\text{ if }x\in[t-\delta,t)\;,\\
0&\text{ if }x\geq t\;.\end{cases}

x
t-\delta
t
t+\delta
h_{1;t;\delta}
h_{1;t+\delta;\delta}

We will use a thrice-differentiable generalisation of
h_{1;t;\delta}
, obtained from setting
\tilde{m}=3
below:

Lemma 13

(Lemma 34 of
43
)
.

Fix any
\tilde{m}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
and
\delta>0
. Then there exists an
\tilde{m}
-times differentiable
\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
function
h_{\tilde{m};\tau;\delta}
such that
h_{\tilde{m};\tau+\delta;\delta}(x)\;\leq\;\mathbb{I}_{\{x>\tau\}}\leq h_{\tilde{m};\tau;\delta}(x)
. For
0\leq r\leq\tilde{m}
, the
r
-th derivative
h^{(r)}_{\tilde{m};\tau;\delta}
is continuous and bounded in absolute value by
\delta^{-r}
. Moreover, for every
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,
h^{(\tilde{m})}_{\tilde{m};\tau;\delta}
satisfies that

\displaystyle|h^{(\tilde{m})}_{\tilde{m};\tau;\delta}(x)-h^{(\tilde{m})}_{\tilde{m};\tau;\delta}(y)|\;\leq\;C_{\tilde{m},\epsilon}\,\delta^{-(\tilde{m}+\epsilon)}\,|x-y|^{\epsilon}\;,

with respect to the constant
C_{\tilde{m},\epsilon}=\binom{\tilde{m}}{\lfloor\tilde{m}/2\rfloor}(\tilde{m}+1)^{\tilde{m}+\epsilon}
.

The approximation error of (
37
) by (
38
), for
h_{t}
of the form above, can be measured by the probability of
g(W)
lying in a small neighbourhood of the threshold
t
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(|g(W)-t|\leq\delta)\;.

(39)

A control on (
39
) is known as an anti-concentration inequality
72
,
16
,
10
:

\bullet

If an anti-concentration inequality for
g(W)
is available, we can translate an upper bound on the distance of smooth functions (
38
) into a direct bound on the distance of c.d.f. (
37
);

\bullet

Even if an anti-concentration inequality for
g(W)
is not available,
if
t
is a point of continuity of
g(W)
, as
\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}
, (
39
) vanishes. Therefore if (
38
) converges to zero, and if
g(W)
converges in distribution to some limit, we get that
g(V)
asymptotically converges to the same limit.

We are ready to state our general universality result under local dependence.

Theorem 14

(Universality under local dependence)
.

Let
g:\mathbb{R}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a thrice-differentiable function, and denote

\displaystyle m_{N}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m}{N^4}$}}

and

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\rm Var}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;N\,\|\text{\rm Var}[V]-I_{m}\|_{\infty}\;.

Then for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle|{\mathbb{P}}(g(V)>t)-{\mathbb{P}}(g(W)>t)|

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\inf_{\delta>0}\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\epsilon_{\rm Var}}{2}$}}\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(\varphi_1(g))^2}{\delta^2}$}}\,+\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\varphi_2(g)}{\delta}$}}\Big)\,+\,{\mathbb{P}}(|g(\xi)-t|\leq\delta)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ 4}{3 \sqrt{m_N}}$}}\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(\varphi_1(g))^3}{\delta^3}$}}\,+\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3 \varphi_1(g) \, \varphi_2(g)}{\delta^2}$}}\,+\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\varphi_3(g)}{\delta}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\,\times\,\max_{i\leq m}\max\big\{\|V_{i}\|_{L_{4}}^{3},\|W_{i}\|_{L_{4}}^{3},3^{\frac{3}{4}}\big\}\Big\}\;.

Suppose in addition that
g
is a polynomial function with degree at most
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
. Then there exists a universal constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\;\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|{\mathbb{P}}(g(V)>t)-{\mathbb{P}}(g(W)>t)|

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\,\Big((\varphi_{1}(g))^{2}\,\epsilon_{\rm Var}^{\frac{1}{2\kappa+1}}\,+\,\varphi_{2}(g)\,\epsilon_{\rm Var}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2\kappa+1}}\Big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C \kappa}{\| g(\xi) \|_{L_2}^{1/\kappa}}$}}\,\max\Big\{m_{N}^{-\frac{1}{2(3\kappa+1)}}\,,\,\epsilon_{\rm Var}^{\frac{1}{2\kappa+1}}\Big\}\;.

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ 4}{3}$}}\,\Big((\varphi_{1}(g))^{3}\,m_{N}^{-\frac{1}{2(3\kappa+1)}}\,+\,3\varphi_{1}(g)\,\varphi_{2}(g)\,m_{N}^{-\frac{\kappa+1}{2(3\kappa+1)}}\,+\,\varphi_{3}(g)\,m_{N}^{-\frac{2\kappa+1}{2(3\kappa+1)}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\;\times\;\max_{i\leq m}\max\big\{\|V_{i}\|_{L_{4}}^{3},\|W_{i}\|_{L_{4}}^{3},3^{\frac{3}{4}}\big\}\;.

Remark 7

(Extensions)
.

We note the following straightforward extensions:

(a)

The second bound above also extends to estimators that are approximately polynomial in the
L_{2}
-norm; see
45
;

(b)

The assumption that
\mathbb{E}[W]=0
and
\text{\rm Var}[W]=I_{m}
is mainly for convenience. To relax them to arbitrary mean and a positive-definite
\text{\rm Var}[W]
, it suffices to replace
g({\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,})
by
g(\text{\rm Var}[W]^{-1/2}({\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,}-\mathbb{E}[W]))
and measure the local dependence of the random vector

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[W]^{-1/2}(V-\mathbb{E}[W])

instead of
V
;

(c)

The assumption that
\mathbb{E}[V]=\mathbb{E}[W]
can be relaxed at the cost of an additional error term that captures mean mismatch, analogous to
\epsilon_{\rm Var}
. This is achieved by applying a first-order Taylor expansion for an appropriately chosen smooth function
\gamma_{\delta}
(defined in the proof of
Theorem

14
below):

\displaystyle\;|\mathbb{E}[\gamma_{\delta}(V)-\gamma_{\delta}(V-\mathbb{E}[V]+\mathbb{E}[W])]|

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\msum_{i=1}^{m}\;\sup\nolimits_{\theta\in[0,1]}\,\mathbb{E}|\partial_{i}\gamma_{\delta}(V-\theta(\mathbb{E}[V]-\mathbb{E}[W]))|\,\times\,|\mathbb{E}[V_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}]|\;.

Up to a redefinition of
\varphi_{1}(g)
to include additional interpolation paths, the mean mismatch will introduce an additional requirement that

\displaystyle\sqrt{m}\,\|\mathbb{E}[V]-\mathbb{E}[W]\|_{\infty}\,\varphi_{1}(g)\;=\;o(1)\;;

(d)

The
\|{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,}\|_{\infty}
norm in the definition of
\epsilon_{\rm Var}
can lead to additional logarithmic dependence on
m
. By inspecting the proof of
Theorem

14
, it is easy to see that the same result holds with
\epsilon_{\rm Var}
replaced by

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\rm Var}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{m}$}}\msum_{i\leq m}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{|\cM^V_i|}$}}\msum_{j\in\mathcal{M}^{V}_{i}}\big|\mathbb{E}[V_{i}V_{j}]-\mathbb{I}_{\{i=j\}}\big|\;.

We do not state these extensions in full for simplicity of presentation.

The proof of
Theorem

14
is by applying the next result to both
V
and
W
together with
Section

E.1
for approximating an indicator by a smooth function.

Lemma 15

(Universality with respect to smooth functions)
.

Let
\delta>0
and
h_{t;\delta}
be given as in
Section

E.1
with
\tilde{m}=3
and
g:\mathbb{R}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a thrice-differentiable function. Then

\displaystyle\;\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\,|\mathbb{E}[h_{t;\delta}(g(V))-h_{t;\delta}(g(\xi))]|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{N}{2}$}}\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(\varphi_1(g))^2}{\delta^2}$}}\,+\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\varphi_2(g)}{\delta}$}}\Big)\,\|\text{\rm Var}[V]-I_{m}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 N^2}{3 \sqrt{m}}$}}\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(\varphi_1(g))^3}{\delta^3}$}}\,+\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3 \varphi_1(g) \, \varphi_2(g)}{\delta^2}$}}\,+\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\varphi_3(g)}{\delta}$}}\Big)\max_{i\leq m}\max\big\{\|V_{i}\|_{L_{4}}^{3},3^{\frac{3}{4}}\big\}\;.

The proof of
Section

E.1
follows a similar recipe as
27
, who prove Gaussian universality of the risk of high-dimensional logistic regression on data that exhibit data-wise block dependent and mixing structure and coordinate-wise local dependence structure. The main difference is that our random variables for comparison do not have exactly matching variance nor local dependency structures.

Proof of
Section

E.1
.

Denote the
\mathbb{R}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
function
\gamma_{\delta}=h_{t;\delta}\circ g
and
e_{i}
as the
i
-th standard basis vector in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
. Also denote
\langle{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,},{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,}\rangle
as the Euclidean inner product defined in the corresponding Euclidean space of the arguments. We first use the interpolation path
I(s)=\sqrt{s}V+\sqrt{1-s}\xi
to write

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\gamma_{\delta}(V)-\gamma_{\delta}(\xi)]|\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big|\mint_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{s}\gamma_{\delta}(I(s))]\big|\,ds\Big|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big|\mint_{0}^{1}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\msum_{i=1}^{m}\big\langle\partial\gamma_{\delta}(I(s))\,,\,e_{i}\big\rangle\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{V_i}{2\sqrt{s}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\xi_i}{2\sqrt{1-s}}$}}\Big)\Big]\,ds\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mint_{0}^{1}\,\underbrace{\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\msum_{i=1}^{m}\,\big\langle\partial\gamma_{\delta}(I(s))\,,\,e_{i}\big\rangle\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{V_i}{2\sqrt{s}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\xi_i}{2\sqrt{1-s}}$}}\Big)\Big]\Big|}_{\eqqcolon Q(s)}\,ds\;.

For convenience we shall define

\displaystyle I^{\mathcal{M}^{V}_{i}}(s)\;\coloneqq\;I(s)-I^{(i)}(s,0)\;=\;I^{(i)}(s,1)-I^{(i)}(s,0)\;.

By a second-order Taylor expansion with the integral remainder, we obtain that almost surely

\displaystyle\big\langle

\displaystyle\partial\gamma_{\delta}(I(s))\,,\,e_{i}\big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\partial\gamma_{\delta}\Big(I^{(i)}(s,0)+I^{\mathcal{M}^{V}_{i}}(s)\Big)\,,\,e_{i}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\;=\;\big\langle\partial\gamma_{\delta}\big(I^{(i)}(s,0)\big)\,,\,e_{i}\big\rangle+\Big\langle\partial^{2}\gamma_{\delta}\big(I^{(i)}(s,0)\big)\,,\,e_{i}\otimes I^{\mathcal{M}^{V}_{i}}(s)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\mint_{0}^{1}\Big\langle\partial^{3}\gamma_{\delta}\big(I^{(i)}(s,\theta)\big)\,,\,e_{i}\otimes I^{\mathcal{M}^{V}_{i}}(s)\otimes I^{\mathcal{M}^{V}_{i}}(s)\Big\rangle\,(1-\theta)^{2}\,d\theta

\displaystyle\;=\;\big\langle\partial\gamma_{\delta}\big(I^{(i)}(s,0)\big)\,,\,e_{i}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\msum_{j\in\mathcal{M}^{V}_{i}}\big\langle\partial^{2}\gamma_{\delta}\big(I^{(i)}(s,0)\big)\,,\,e_{i}\otimes e_{j}\big\rangle\,\big(\sqrt{s}\;V_{j}\,+\,\sqrt{1-s}\;\xi_{j}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\msum_{j,l\in\mathcal{M}^{V}_{i}}\,\bigg(\mint_{0}^{1}\Big\langle\partial^{3}\gamma_{\delta}\big(I^{(i)}(s,\theta)\big)\,,\,e_{i}\otimes e_{j}\otimes e_{l}\Big\rangle\,(1-\theta)^{2}\,d\theta

\displaystyle\hskip 85.00012pt\,\times\,\big(\sqrt{s}\;V_{j}\,+\,\sqrt{1-s}\;\xi_{j}\big)\big(\sqrt{s}\;V_{l}\,+\,\sqrt{1-s}\;\xi_{l}\big)\bigg)\;.

Plugging this into
Q(s)
, while noting that
\mathbb{E}[V_{i}]=\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i}]=0
and
